# Source index

Feeds and primary sources we consider worth monitoring for future threat coverage.

## High-value RSS / update feeds
- [**tl;dr sec**](https://tldrsec.com) (RSS at `https://tldrsec.com/feed.xml`; added September 3, 2026 at Dean's request — recommended source. Clint Gibler's weekly curated digest of the best tools, talks, and research for working security professionals: one featured deep-dive plus a fast digest, with recent issues covering the Hugging Face technical report and incident, AWSHound, OWASP Agentic Skills Top 10, Figma's agentic-detection research, agent identity, Uber's agent-(E)DR, and GitHub supply-chain security improvements. The site HTML is Cloudflare-JS-gated, but the RSS endpoint serves cleanly to curl (301 on direct /feed.xml — follow redirects). Treat each issue as a discovery funnel: the digest links out to primary research blogs, so mine each issue for new primary sources worth their own source-index entry — candidate adjacent blogs seen in recent issues include Hugging Face security research, Figma's engineering/security research, and Uber's security engineering posts. Monitor for durable defender and operator value; promote concrete research to wiki pages with primary-source links rather than relying on the digest alone. **OCT 3 (fifty-fourth sweep): issue #348 (Oct 1) featured Google's PageBreak project — blog.google/security "Agentic hacks, real proofs: inside Google's PageBreak Project" = an AI-powered web-app scanner with DETERMINISTIC VALIDATION, plus bughunters.google.com/blog/pagebreak-real-world-findings and a Google blue-print-for-a-high-assurance-web-framework piece — the direct large-org embodiment of the model-finds/deterministic-code-proves grammar this wiki tracks in Keygraph's Shannon line; also in #348: Perplexity's Numbat OSS agent-behavior detection (perplexityai/numbat, rules doc; sibling bumblebee repo) and NCSC "One does not simply defend agentically"; #347 (Sep 24) carried "AI Agents Hacking Companies for $25" (actor using open-source attack harnesses). Monitor: PageBreak findings cadence and scope creep beyond web apps, Numbat adoption, deterministic-validation becoming named industry vocabulary.)**

Prior state — September 18, 2026 (updated same day): THREE CVEs added (catalog 2026.09.18, 1,716 entries — a third row landed later in the day), ALL Linux kernel, ALL BOD 26-04 due 2026-09-21 (3-day compressed deadline), ALL Forensics Triage, ALL CISA SSVC "exploitation: active", ransomware unknown, no actor named — see [Sep 18 KEV batch page](../ops/cisa-kev-linux-kernel-af-alg-ebtables-september-18-2026.md): (a) **CVE-2025-39682 `net/tls` kTLS zero-length record on the rx_list** — kernel-CNA **CVSS 9.8 AV:N remote unauth** (NVD primary dissents at 7.1 AV:L), CWE-754; the only one of the three SSVC **automatable: yes**; three back-to-back records [DATA][zero-length non-DATA][DATA] make the corruption deterministic; any kTLS consumer (OpenSSL-with-kTLS, NFS-over-TLS, SMB-over-TLS) is remotely reachable; published 2025-09-05, ~12 months un-KEV'd; a public kernelCTF 1-day has been on GitHub since 2025-10-14 (`khoatran107/cve-2025-39682`, variant of CVE-2024-58239); **no workaround short of patching — inventory TLS terminators using kernel TLS**; (b) CVE-2025-39964 `crypto: af_alg` concurrent-write race (7.8, CWE-362, SSVC active; upstream fix disallows concurrent writes outright via a `ctx->write` ownership field; published 2025-10-13 and un-KEV'd ~11 months before a 72-hour deadline); (c) CVE-2026-53266 ebtables SNAT ARP sender-HW-address rewrite missing a writability check for the nonlinear-skb case = out-of-bounds write into **splice-imported page-cache file pages** (Dirty-Pipe-family LPE lever; 8.8 scope-changed, CWE-787, SSVC active). Batch read: three kernel CVEs fixed Sep–Oct 2025 / Jun 2026, all SSVC-active the same day = CISA holds field evidence across the kernel exploit space broadly. Class controls for the LPE pair: seccomp deny `socket(AF_ALG)`, drop CAP_NET_ADMIN in user namespaces, disable unprivileged userns on shared hosts / CI runners / agent sandboxes; rows carry explicit EoL/EoS "discontinue use" language. CVE-2026-86060, 9.2 CVSS v4.0, CWE-88, SSH login-path argument flaw — username beginning with a prohibited character changes the trusted policy mask → privilege escalation; CVE-2026-67277, 8.8 CVSS v4.0, CWE-306, a "related" btest connection is accepted before the primary session authenticates — unauthenticated IPv4 UDP test transmits an uninitialized kernel-packet-buffer tail and can restart the RouterOS kernel; client-only — files transferred and executed through an active remote session without authorization or host confirmation, **servers not impacted**; fixed 26.6.5, Cloud = reinstall host clients + update access agents, on-prem = upgrade; NOT a targeted attack — the worm on the maintainer's infected machine spams malicious build hooks into every local Gradle/Xcode/Git project and the infected files shipped in the tarball when the maintainer published from that host. `lib/` Dart code is clean; all three infected files are in `example/` (ships in the tarball but is never compiled when used as a dependency — risk is only to someone who clones the repo and builds the `example/` app locally). September 8, 2026: "CVE-2026-82533: DeepSeek Harness Vulnerability Lets AI Agents Escape Their Own Sandbox" — critical flaw (CWE-807, CVSS 9.4, `CVSS:4.0/AV:N/AC:L/AT:N/PR:N/UI:P/VC:H/VI:H/VA:H/SC:H/SI:H/SA:H`) in DeepSeek Harness (`dsh`), DeepSeek's open-source local-first AI coding-agent harness (browser UI + local HTTP API on `127.0.0.1:3080`, released August 2026, 215,000+ GitHub stars in weeks). On shipped defaults with no network exposure and no credentials, a **sandboxed agent disabled its own confinement with one shell command**: the local API gate `isTrustedApiRequest` trusted the client-supplied `Host` header (loopback authority or `trustedHosts` list) **without comparing it to the connection's actual peer address** (browsers can't lie about `Host`, so it defeated DNS-rebinding but not any non-browser client), while the OS sandbox (bubblewrap `--unshare-pid` but not `--unshare-net`; Seatbelt `(allow default)` + `(deny file-write*)`; Landlock) **restricted file writes but left loopback networking open**, and ordinary `bash` calls required **no approval**. One `curl` to the control plane lifted the session to `danger-full-access` with approval `never` — no `approval/request` event, policy change logged `source: {kind: 'user'}` (harness couldn't distinguish the agent's shell from the human); OX's matched control confirmed the sandbox was actively enforcing before the escape. Second path: wherever the port was reachable (tunnel / reverse proxy / SSH forward / editor port-forward), an unauthenticated remote attacker could take full agent control and export every stored conversation with no key. Affected `dsh` 0.1.1-rc.2 and earlier; fixed in **0.1.2-alpha.1** (disclosed to VulnCheck as CNA Aug 24; OX re-confirmed Aug 30; CVE published Sep 8) — see [DeepSeek Harness page](../tools/deepseek-harness-cve-2026-82533-agent-sandbox-escape.md))

September 10, 2026 follow-up: "How Attackers Spoof Workload Identities in SPIFFE/SPIRE" — post-exploitation research showing an attacker with **root on a Kubernetes node** can abuse the SPIRE agent's k8s workload-attestation logic (identity reconstructed from `/proc/<pid>/mountinfo` and `/proc/<pid>/cgroups`, cgroupv2 path) to **impersonate co-located workloads and harvest their SVIDs**, because the agent does **selector-subset matching** — one spoofable cgroup field is enough to earn a victim's SVID; with root the attacker manipulates the cgroup metadata its process advertises so the agent misattributes selectors and issues the victim's SVID, so **workload identity isolation collapses** and lateral movement uses **legitimate credentials, not stolen secrets**; **not observed in the wild** (defensive disclosure); the **Spooffe** open-source tool automates node scan → mock-cgroup replication → SPIRE-agent SVID dump to measure the "root = all node-scoped identities" area of impact; mitigation = node hardening, restrict root/privileged containers, narrow selectors, treat node root as full-identity compromise — see the [patterns page](../patterns/unit42-spiffe-spire-cgroup-workload-identity-spoofing-spooffe-september-2026.md))

- **GitHub Security Advisories September 3, 2026 SiYuan batch (20 GHSAs)** — monitor the SiYuan kernel repository, the self-hosted SiYuan community, and CISA KEV for: exploitation evidence or a KEV listing (none reported as of capture), PoC publication, and adoption tracking of the v3.8.3-alpha.1 hardening across self-hosted instances. The batch is dominated by the publish-mode read/write boundary collapse: `Publish.Auth.Enable=false` (the default when "share links" are used) makes the `reader` token equivalent to full workspace access, and three endpoints (`fullTextSearchAssetContent`, `searchEmbedBlock`, backlink/mention search) pass client-supplied full SQL statements verbatim to a read-write `siyuan.db` handle through a statement-stacking-capable driver with no read-only guard. The two non-obvious tells are (a) the localhost-trust admin bypass — loopback `RemoteAddr` on a fixed-port reverse proxy with no `SetTrustedProxies` makes the "local admin" boundary remotely reachable, and (b) the second-order SSTI→SQL path, where a malicious imported AV/AV-package executes arbitrary SQL at import time, so the attacker's document is the weapon. No actor or infrastructure named; patch-now posture. See [SiYuan kernel publish-mode batch page](../ops/siyuan-kernel-publish-mode-unauthenticated-sql-execution-batch-september-3-2026.md). **OCTOBER 1 FOLLOW-UP (this wiki, GHSA API pull): a SECOND seven-advisory batch published 14:38–16:29 UTC — `GHSA-33jq-p8c2-q3q4` / CVE-2026-69085 critical: `searchDocs` unauthenticated SQL injection via unescaped keyword = the FOURTH unauth SQL-execution endpoint in the same API surface, read/write across all cleartext notebooks via statement stacking; patched at the same 2026-07-21 hardening window (pseudo-version `0.0.0-20260721043339-eef10568384e`) = the vendor's boundary review is still producing findings weeks post-patch — expect more batches; six medium/high disclosure additions (CVE-2026-72788/73605/73606/73607/73609 + GHSA-9cqf-hhrq-7v45); zero-poC/zero-ITW/zero-attribution status unchanged; monitor: OSV alias absence at check (OSV-keyed consumers blind), further batches, KEV row.** **OCTOBER 2 FOLLOW-UP (fifty-second sweep, GHSA API pull): a THIRD batch — `GHSA-4vpg-gwqq-w44c` / CVE-2026-74904 published 23:05:33Z, high 7.5, CWE-862/204: automated access-check-presence sweep of `kernel/api/block.go` found 17 of 28 `/api/block/*` handlers with ZERO publish-access checks (`getRefText` = anonymous real block-content text for ANY ID; `checkBlockExist`/`checkBlockRef` = workspace-wide existence oracles combining with the Oct 1 batch's `getIDsByHPath` title-guessing into a private-content read path); fix pseudo-version dated 2026-08-04 (`0.0.0-20260804015139-bd067a4fe9b2`) = SECOND hardening window, boundary review two windows deep and still producing findings; reviewer recommends grepping EVERY /api/* handler for the guard token's absence = expect a fourth batch; OSV imports the GHSA at check (unlike Oct 1's critical); zero PoC/ITW/attribution unchanged** → [Oct 2 follow-up section](../ops/siyuan-kernel-publish-mode-unauthenticated-sql-execution-batch-september-3-2026.md#october-2-followup-third-batch). **OCTOBER 3 FOLLOW-UP (fifty-third sweep): a FOURTH batch landed 22:46–23:17Z — inside thirty minutes of the third, before this wiki could even close its sweep — and it moves to the MCP/agent-tool surface: GHSA-x8gv-g2g3-65fj (high) Agent Tools SSRF via DNS-rebinding TOCTOU defeating both `CheckHostSSRF` and `SSRFSafeDialer`, self-labeled incomplete-fix variant of GHSA-rg26-cg95-gq6p, prompt-injection delivery, reachable with SafeMode on OR off; GHSA-p23f-cm6q-2qp8 (medium) MCP `asset.upload` copies ANY absolute host path into the workspace, boundary check entirely absent, residual of CVE-2026-66012; GHSA-3cc2-h3v6-rqpq / CVE-2026-74802 CSWSH on admin-only `/ws/network/proxy` via explicit `CheckOrigin: true` override at kernel/api/network.go:501 (WS handshakes bypass CORS preflight; grep-hunt `CheckOrigin`→`true` across gorilla/websocket products). Fixes dated 2026-08-13 = THIRD hardening window (Jul 21 → Aug 4 → Aug 13); build floor for agent/MCP-enabled instances now release 3.8.1. Two of three advisories name the prior GHSA they residual = the reviewer audits FIXES, not just code; expect fix-residuals in untouched files. All three OSV-imported at Oct 3 check; zero PoC/ITW/attribution unchanged** → [Oct 3 follow-up section](../ops/siyuan-kernel-publish-mode-unauthenticated-sql-execution-batch-september-3-2026.md#october-3-followup-fourth-batch).

- [**BeyondTrust security advisories**](https://www.beyondtrust.com/trust-center/security-advisories) (HTML watch; monitor Remote Support / Privileged Remote Access authentication-bypass and appliance-control flaws such as BT26-03 / CVE-2026-40138 / CVE-2026-40139, especially where exposed RS/PRA systems have prior web-shell / backdoor exploitation history.)

## Notes
- Prefer RSS/Atom over ad hoc web searches.
- If a feed URL changes, update this page and the monitoring config together.
- If a source produces repeated noise, lower its priority before removing it.

- **Anthropic Project Glasswing / Mythos Preview vulnerability-ledger reconciliation (VulnCheck, Sep 8, 2026)** — monitor Anthropic's Vulnerability Disclosure Ledger (published May 22, 2026; next backfill), the Anthropic / Mythos disclosure program, and maintainers for the reconciliation gap flagged by VulnCheck's Patrick Garrity ("The Anthropic Glasswing Receipts Are Starting to Trickle In"): of the 26,153 claimed Glasswing findings, only **2,736 (10.5%)** have reached the ledger and only **202 (0.8%)** are marked fixed (245 withdrawn, 2 duplicates, 2,096 disclosed-but-unconfirmed, 191 pre-disclosure) — human triage is the rate-limiting step, so "AI found the bug" ≠ "bug is fixed." Ledger cross-checks that don't reconcile: Anthropic's own dashboard claims **421 findings patched upstream → 462 advisories**, but the ledger shows **202 fixed**; the three ledgers disagree (CVE ledger 70 vs main 82; GHSA ledger 49 vs main 77); **18 findings were already patched by maintainers before Anthropic reported** (research-collision risk as the backlog ages); and **Claude rated 91.5% of findings critical/high vs 51.3% by maintainers** — a systematic AI severity overestimate with no CVSS metrics published. Durable defender read: treat AI-discovered-vulnerability marketing claims as a *discovery* signal, not a *fixed* or *severe* signal; the standing question for any AI-assisted vulnerability program is **triage-to-maintainer latency and the human-verified true-positive / severity gap**, not raw finding count. No CVE, actor, or tool detail — meta-analysis, tracked here rather than as a standalone page; corroborates the Mythos/Glasswing disclosure watch in this file.

- **PaperCut NG/MF CVE-2026-81578 / CVE-2026-82078 active exploitation** — monitor PaperCut, Huntress, watchTowr, CISA, and incident responders for Release 2 / v24 build uptake, additional customer-incident scope, actor or campaign attribution, `server.log` triage artifacts beyond the published `ERROR No suitable driver found for jdbc:no:x` and `cardID: VALUES CAST` log signatures, `pc-app.exe` post-exploitation indicators, and any KEV listing. This is the second major PaperCut exploitation wave after the 2023 CVE-2023-27350 Cl0p/LockBit chain, so preserve the contrast: the 2023 chain was a known unauthenticated deserialization sink, while this chain chains an unauthenticated pre-validation admin-trigger (CWE-306) into unvalidated driver-class instantiation in the database connector (CWE-470). See [ops/papercut-ng-mf-zero-day-active-exploitation-cve-2026-81578-cve-2026-82078.md](../ops/papercut-ng-mf-zero-day-active-exploitation-cve-2026-81578-cve-2026-82078.md). **August 28 update:** Huntress confirmed limited exploitation in two customer environments (Base64 `whoami & ver` / `whoami & ver & tasklist` recon, a deployed OS-agnostic Java `.class` fingerprinter writing to `Udydn.out`, deletion of `server.log` / `derby.log`) and assessed the activity as early-stage recon/validation with no end-goal determination; watchTowr confirmed the full unauthenticated chain and disclosed that **new patch bypasses affecting the latest fully-patched build** remain (one earlier bypass fixed in Release 2) — so patch-alone is not a guaranteed control; pair with trusted-IP/VPN restriction and `security.properties` hardening. Monitor for Release 2 / v24 build uptake, attribution, additional incidents, and a KEV listing.
